# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1983

## Eight Prayers Glorifying the Spiritual Master

**Śrīla Prabhupāda deva-karuṇāṣṭakam**

by Kuśakratha dāsa

**1.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He is the saintly messenger of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the merciful purifier and deliverer of the conditioned souls. He is a perfectly pure devotee of Lord Govindadeva.

**2.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. His mercy has released the incomparable golden lion of Lord Gaurasundar’s mercy, who leaps into the jungle of the Kali-yuga and chases away the various illusions of māyā.

**3.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Having crossed the salt-water ocean, he flooded the desertlike Western countries with the nectarean chanting of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.

**4.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He took the araṇi-sticks\* of hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa and ignited the sacred fire of kṛṣṇa-bhakti in this world.

**5.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. By describing the transcendental form of Lord Kṛṣṇacandra, he protects the people of this world from the horrible, faceless, formless calamity that is the impersonal conception of God.

**6.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. With the weapon of his trascendental purports he mercifully breaks the shackles of karma which bind the conditioned souls to this world of repeated birth and death.

**7.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Compassionate on the distressed living entities wandering through various species forgetful of their real life, he leads them to the blissful lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

**8.** I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He has mercifully changed the future destiny of his followers from what was once inauspicious to the superlative of auspiciousness.

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Tripurāri Swami

Vyāsa-pūja is the day on which we glorify the bona fide spiritual master appearing in disciplic succession from Śrīla Vyāsadeva. The disciplic succession is an unbroken chain through which Vedic knowledge is handed down from spiritual master to disciple. The beginning of that chain is Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who spoke Vedic knowledge to Brahmā from within his heart. Brahmā then spoke it to Nārada, and Nārada revealed the same truth to Vyāsadeva. Vyāsa means Vyāsadeva, the direct disciple of Śrī Nārada Muni and compiler of all Vedic literature. The ripened fruit of the tree of Vedic literature is *Śrīmad-Bhāgavantam*, presented by Vyāsa in his maturity. *Pūjā* means “workship.” We worship Śrīla Vyāsa Muni or anyone who appears in the unbroken chain of disciplic succession from Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Such a person, well-versed in the Vedic conclusion and spiritually situated by dint of unalloyed devotional service, is a bona fide spiritual master.

On his appearance, the spiritual master is honored as “as good as God” for having humbly accepted the service of representing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He has not concocted something new in the name of transcendental knowledge, fashionably suited for modern times.

Transcendental knowledge is eternally existing, and if the *guru* has done anything wonderful, it is that he repeats that knowledge without adulteration. Everything he says is supported by scriptural reference. This repetition, however, is not parrotlike. Rather, it is repetition with elucidation. Although the *guru* is one-his message is the same as the previous *ācārya*-he is also different in that he finds novel ways and means to potently reveal that knowledge according to time and circumstance. Just as the lotus is pleased to blossom, revealing its beauty, through the rays of the sun, so Vedic knowledge and its objects, Śrī Kṛṣṇa are pleased to reveal themselves through the words of the spiritual master unto the disciple who worships him with faith and love.

For those of us who are sold out to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda, Vyāsa-pūjā means everything. It is a time of giving everything, a time of rededication to serve unceasingly. It means orchards of fruit and gardens of flower offerings, with decorations defeating the heavens. It means hundreds of preparations to be offered at noon after staying up all night. It means tearful eyes as we sing in joyful gravity-*saṁsāra-dāvānalalīḍha-loka*. It means embarrassment, as we all sit before His Divine Grace and one by one read or speak our offerings, feeling our impurities all too present after still another year of service. It means Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are yours, and you are ours. The meaning is something found in an intimate moment, an inside secret, so to speak, that cannot remain as such, but must be broadcast to the entire world, that everyone may join with us on this day of celebration. And rightfully so, for Śrīla Prabhupāda has given life to the world through his personal example and through his Bhaktivedanta purports. He is truly the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Tripurāri Swami

## Introduction by Sundararūpa dāsa Goswami

Vyāsa-pūjā offerings chronicle the evolution of our relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda through his instructions. Those instructions are alive with his presence, invoking us no longer to simple dwell upon the past, but rather to envision the future expanded through his books, his movement, his devotees, his projects, etc. It is not that this book should just fade away, repeating itself into oblivion, as if eulogizing an ordinary man.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disappearance had launched ISKCON into an era of uncertainty, never given so much forethought while he was present, at least other than by himself. Now, that era has past. Are not the misadventure of our deviations bringing us back to the purity of his instructions? Indeed, ISKCON has not only survived its most critical moments but has continued with unprecedented success.

More than anything else, Śrīla Prabhupāda was eager to hear about such successful preaching. Therefore, this transcendental yearbook should hallmark his continued achievements through his empowered disciples and granddisciples, by specifically attributing to him the credit for all literatures distributed, devotees made, new centers opened and other preaching goals achieved. Further acknowledgement must also be given to those revelations by which he enables us to project the future expansion of Kṛṣṇa consciousness with a great sense for the transcendental destiny foreseen by Lord Caitanya. Express commitment in our offerings to these projections links our efforts more directly with him, certainly serving as an impetus to accelerate our progress toward fulfilling his desires.

When Śrīla Prabhupāda was physically present, whatever successful breakthroughs in preaching were achieved in one place were immediately communicated to him, and he would then broadcast all such good news everywhere else by letter or as he travelled. Success was not only encouraged but also shared. Everyone was very eager to hear from him about which were the next steps forward in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In this way, his continuous, dynamic adjustments removed all obstacles to our progress, allowing our success to expand naturally, never statically forzen by historical compromise.

His vision was global, always thinking of the world first. Although we may have exclusively preferred local development, he has exploited our internationality by engaging us in worldwide cooperative ventrues such as the Māyāpur project.

Of course, to a part of Lord Caitanya’s great mission, each of us has his sacred right, but not to unnecessarily create more diversity, when sufficient already exists. In the material world, diversity never lacks, only unity. In contrast, within the anticipated “house” that Śrīla Prabhupāda built, there is unity in diversity.

Previously we depended upon Śrīla Prabhupāda directly for this unity, but now we should compensate for his absence by our common understanding of his instructions, which he himself summarized as follows:

Preaching is the essence,  
Purity is the force,  
Books are the basis,  
And utility is the principle

Naturally, if we are determined to cooperate for the purpose of following these instructions, we will avoid interpreting any one of them at the expense of another. Thus embracing all of his instructions together, absolute comprehension will be achieved, the whole being greater than the sum of its parts. The combined effect is the additional element we can call our love to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions.

Understood in this way, his instructions can unite our preaching efforts now just as he personally did when he was present, although now more initiative is required on our part. We must, therefore, become more attached to following his instructions than anything else. This attachment may take our entire lifetime to develop, or it can be done in just eighteen days if we are serious.

Being serious means to manifest our love for Śrīla Prabhupāda and his instructions as the predominant influence in this world. Everyone should recognize that only these instructions can save them from the natural consequences of material life. The present condition of the world dictates urgency, making it imperative that we achieve this common goal that unites our movement as soon as possible.

“A devotee is an opportunist,” Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “and must preach boldly, as if hunting a rhinoceros.” Otherwise, if we miss our chance, then history will credit others, our predecessors’ misfortune. They could not understand this secret of his success, because they did not know who he was and still is, above all-the beloved of his spiritual master and of his disciples.

That unlimited capacity of his for loving, spiritual relationships, explains how he accomplished alone what now requires all of us together. In his generation, all truth how to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness was fully revealed to him, the one who showed the most love for his *Guru* Mahārāja by following the instruction to preach in the West. Yet, his mandate for us is that our love for him is proven only if we cooperate, implying that, individually, we cannot do the same as he did. Unless we combine our efforts, we have no hope to carry on that which Śrīla Prabhupāda has already established singlehandedly.

Although this may seem contradictory, it is consistent, nonetheless, because love for the spiritual master is the common denominator. *Acintya-bhedābheda-tattva*. Furthermore, expressions of such love can have no superficial medium, but must take that form requested by the beloved spiritual master himself: *dharmaṁ hi sākṣād bhagavat-praṇītam*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda cannot be loved alone, just as we never find Kṛṣṇa alone, nor perform *saṅkīrtana* alone. We love Śrīla Prabhupāda, therefore we love each other. That is the way to love everyone. Love by its very nature cannot remain incomplete. And we cannot imagine what will be the historical and global impact of our combined love for Śrīla Prabhupāda-nor how pleased he will be when he hears about it in our future Vyāsa-pūjā offerings.

Sundararūpa dāsa Goswami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the G.B.C.

## Atreya Ṛṣi dāsa

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Time and time again you spent numerous hours explaining this sublime philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to me. And time and time again you turned to me, like a mother begging of her naughty child, “Are you convinced? Do you now appreciate that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the highest philosophy?” In my youth and arrogance I kept quiet, with doubtful eyes.

And then in Tehran, in the car, off Dowlat, you turned to me and boldly stated, “I have come here just to save you. I traveled here to give you my mercy.” And again I fixed my eyes at your lotus feet, tormented by the agony of disbelief.

Now I beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to allow me to serve your disciples, and all your disciples to come, in a worthless effort to repay all that mercy you unconditionally bestowed upon me.

*Servant of your servants,*  
Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

## Balavanta dāsa

**My Head at His Feet**

Remembrance of his visage  
Is the source of pure joy,  
And the mere thought of him  
Is enough to banish the face of fear.

The sound of his voice  
Itself contains realization,  
And his direction and example  
Are the essence of religion.

Beside his teachings,  
The brilliance of the sun is paled,  
And the soothing of the summer moon  
Cannot compete with the solace of his smile.

The sparkling mountain stream  
Is no rival for his pure heart,  
And the paid of violent death  
Is preferable to the loss of his blessings.

And when the inevitable grip of death  
Smothers the breath and sends pangs of remorse through the heart,  
When family, friends, wealth and position desert us one by one,  
And darkness envelops the consciousness and panic grips the brain.

He will stand before us still, in death, as he has in life,  
A sure and steady hand, a leader, friend and guide,  
And may I at that time,  
In ecstatic trance of devotion, once again

My lowly head bow down at the altar of his lotus feet.

Balavanta dāsa

## Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

**Thoughts on Your Mission**

One in whose heart rests ambition  
Will he be satisfied even in becoming *guru*?

As zero has no meaning without following one,  
One who stands apart from ISKON loses all value and becomes  
Untouchable-even if a *guru*.

You do not bless by simply giving a title or position  
But by giving the ability to fulfill it.  
Your ISKON is not a costume  
House.

First let us serve you exactly as you have shown and requested.  
Then we can consider our own creative intelligence, plans and  
Individuality.

Amassing collections for maintenance without flooding the world with  
Books and prasādam.  
One becomes a glutton, whose veins being blocked succumbs to failure  
of heart.

Although the scientists are tightly binding the world with their  
Impersonalist conclusions, we still seem to be coming up short.  
Please provide us that self-effulgent rope which will drag the world from  
these depths of voidism.

When sufficient brāhmaṇas uphold the regulative principles without  
Difficulty, then varṇāśrama can unfold brilliantly under ISKCON’s Banner.  
Otherwise, what can vaiśyas and śūdras hope to accomplish?

Out temples and zones are transcendental embassies, so long as we work  
For the essence of all temples-Śrī Māyāpur-dhāma.  
Otherwise they will simply become extensions of the bodily concept of  
life and be the cause of creating friends and enemies.

In Śrī Māyāpur-dhāma there will be no  
conception of “I” and “mine”.  
On this basis each country can build its home and manifest a perfect  
United Nations- “One nation under God and Prabhupāda.”

Due to the past, the lawyers must represent you in the courts.  
Now let us learn once and for all how we should represent you properly,  
so you do not have to stand for our negligence.

May we learn from you how to avoid blind justice or foolish mercy,  
Both of which create havoc in the house.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know what dangers lie ahead-and you are  
safely waiting on the other side.  
It will be especially sweet to meet you-having successfully met the challenge.

Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda!*

IN YOUR several written instructions to this most unworthy servant regarding presenting the Kṛṣṇa conscious thesis on “Life Comes from Life,” you revealed, “If we can prove that life comes from life, or the soul is from the Supersoul, then all other things can be brought into serious consideration. So you try to prove that chemical combination can never bring about life. This is our main argument. If we can prove this particular subject matter, that the soul cannot be manufactured by combination of chemicals, then gradually we can prove that Vedic knowledge is perfect, while other sources of knowledge by speculation and imagination are all wrong.” … “Our mission is to turn the people to become devotees so that they can see Krishna. So we have got a great mission. You are one of the strong pillars of the mission. So I have all confidence in you. Follow strictly all our rules and regulations. Be fully strong to push on this cult amongst the educated scientific circle. That you are getting many speaking invitations is a good indication. Take advantage of this. Krishna is asking you to do this job.”

“As far as the College and University Lecture Party is concerned, that is very important.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you also pointed out very strongly that the Bhaktivedanta Institute preaching party in all universities and other institutions throughout the world is for making scientific presenations of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This party is to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness as the genuine scientific movement of the world, exposing the so-called scientists as nothing but speculators. “This party is our most important preaching arm with which we will be able to destroy the bogus speculation and cheating which goes under the banner of scientific advancement.” …. “So I am looking forward to when you shall actually begin these activities.” …. “ If you can convince one man, that will be sufficient to save millions.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is boundless. Without your divine association this world is completely meaningless. Your mercy is all that I am made of.

One this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I would like to submit under your divine lotus feet that we are making a humble beginning to execute your divine order from this very day. You have already sent so many nice and intelligent devotees for this program. All of them are extremely enthusiastic. I would also like to invite anyone who would like to participate in this most important preaching program. Let us make sure that all the students and scholars all over the world sing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s glories. Let us all make sure that the mundane and transient scientific knowledge is replaced by the eternal principles of bhāgavata science. We are one hundred percent sure that this will be most pleasing to Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhaktitirtha Swami

**My Promises to You**

*sādhu-saṅga-kṛpā kimvā kṛṣṇera kṛpāya*  
*kāmādi 'duḥsaṅga' chāḍi' śuddha-bhakti pāya*

**“One is elevated to the platform of devotional service by the mercy of a Vaiṣṇava, the bona fide spiritual master, and by the special mercy of Kṛṣṇa. On that platform, one gives up all material desires and the association of unwanted people. Thus one is elevated to the platform of pure devotional service.”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a germ in total darkness, infecting everything that I could contaminate.  
I promised to you if you would uplift me, I would cease these cowardly attacks.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I stood for thousands of years as a tree. I was tortured by the fierce weather and covered by the defecation of the birds.  
I promised to you if you would uplift me, never again would I present myself in such an indecent fashion.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a worm living in stool.  
I promised if you would uplift me, I would give up this most obnoxious attempt for enjoyment.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a scaly fish residing in the depths of the ocean.  
I promised if you would allow me to see light and to breath fresh air that I would forever come out of hiding.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was crawling on my belly as an insect.  
I tried to swallow anything smaller than myself.

I promised to you if you would uplift me, I would give up this ignoble life-style.  
My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was an animal walking “on all fours,”  
I dined every day on a meal of raw flesh and blood.

I promised to you if you would uplift me that I would stop terrorizing the weaker living entities.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was an infant, suffocating in the womb of my mother, choking on vile odors and being eaten by worms swimming in blood.  
I promised if you would let me escape, I would never take birth again.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a young boy experiencing such great proverty.  
I promised to you if you would uplift me, I would dedicate my life to helping others.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a teenager living on revolutionary ideology and full of hate.  
I promised if you would uplift me, I would lay down my weapons.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a flamboyant college student, living on intoxication, searching for the perfect girl.  
I promised , after I found her, that if you would now take her away I would be eternally grateful.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a young adult, only interested in occultism and mystic power.  
I promised to you that if you would free me from these entanglements, I would have a proper mind to understand what is truth.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a selfish new bhakta.  
I promised if you would give me initiation that I would never break the “four regs.”

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once I was a “puffed-up” incompetent devotee.  
I promised if you would make me a brāhmaṇa, I would preach he śāstras nicely.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once the devotees told me to take more responsibility and to give up this artificial humility.  
So I promised to remain celibate for the rest of my life and enter into the sannyāsa-āśrama.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, now I am pretending or acting as a GBC.  
Now I promise I will see that everyone in these fifty countries, on this dark continent, gets a chance to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and to know that Kṛṣṇa is God and that you are one of His greatest, most confidential devotees.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, today I thank you for uplifting me from being a germ.  
I beg forgiveness, because I still have all of my promises to fulfill.

Your most fallen disciple.  
Bhaktitīrtha Swami

## Bhavānanda Goswami

Meditation is simple if we can fathom who you are,  
That you are note of mortal men but coming from afar,  
That your birth is not forced, like ours, committed by our sins,  
But happens by sweet love’s command . His mercy incarnation.  
While our desire is always first, no thought for Kṛṣṇa’s will  
You will for Him, no thought of self. His message to fulfill.  
The example of your life, so flawless, sweet and pure,  
Serves as the beacon light of truth, without which we are unsure.  
“Oh! If Prabhupāda were only here,” we cry in agony,  
Forgetting that before you went, you left us books by which to see.  
Everything is there; we must simply turn the pages-  
The knowledge of eternal life from you, the topmost of the sages.  
Four your life was meant for nothing else, from your birth to your departure,  
But for giving to your children the love on which to nurture.  
That love we keep within our hearts, the example of your life,  
And pray to you, dear Prabhupāda, to free us from internal strife.  
For that would be the worst of sins, to jeopardize your mission  
And cause such harm that all the world could not recovery from the lesson.  
So this is our fervent prayer: “You please guide us from within  
For unity to stay the course to save the world from sin.”

Bhavānanda Goswami

## Brahmānanda Swami

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
For taking the words of your *Guru* Mahārāja so seriously.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
For going out from Vṛndāvana, the stage for Lord Kṛṣṇa eternal pastimes, of which you are a part.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for being so determined that you journeyed to America by the only means available-a boat.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for risking your frail life and being able to somehow withstand two heart attacks that would have felled any ordinary person.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for not being content to sit in living rooms and parlors chatting with middle America.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for not even being content to be sheltered in the swanky yoga āśramas of uptown Manhattan.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for following Lord Kṛṣṇa as He led you to us, who were waiting in the depths of the Lower East Side just for you, although who could have known it?

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
For standing under a tree in a park, speaking out about Kṛṣṇa and the eternal science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for sitting with eyes closed, playing a bongo drum and singing in deep ecstasy Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Hare Hare, which made us get up and dance for an entire afternoon and forever.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for miraculously transforming a tiny storefront into the spiritual world where everything was made of effulgent touchstone, filled with wish-fulling kalpa-vṛkṣā trees and hundreds of thousands of surabhi cows yielding all spiritual desires, where every one of us was trying for the first time to be a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for cooking kichaurīs, your personal favorite, and offering them to Lord Kṛṣṇa and then giving out the remnants with your own golden hand onto the plate of me, a stranger, and into my heart and soul.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for noticing, that the new boy Bruce had washed your pots and pans better than the others had done.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for handing us a string of wooden beads that connected us to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is more than God, because He is a person, and He lives, and you taught us how to live with Him.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for writing your books by sitting in front of your metal box and at first typing out the words on the hand typewriters we gave you, which we had used in college.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for handing over the manuscripts to us to publish and for giving your Back to Godhead magazine for us to write after you had delivered to us your seed-planting lectures on the *Bhagava-gitā* As It Is, the great *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and the sublime *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for suddenly going out from our warm little group far away to California, thus making us stand up by ourselves, forcing us to walk in your lotus footsteps in service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, even though we had been crippled for lifetimes and had never walked before.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for coming back to the storefront to perform a unique ceremony over two shiny brass Deities of Radha and Kṛṣṇa, then ordering us to bathe, dress, feed and worship Them every day in your upstairs apartment as you left to go preach in some other place.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for again returning to America, after even another heart stroke had taken you back to India, where your health was most acclimated; still you came back to us to try again and to risk your life yet again.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for instructing your new eager devotees how to preach, perform sanṅkīrtana on the streets, distribute your books, canvass people, worship in the temple and observe festivals and manage things properly, and thus establish your Angeles world headquarters.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for chastising me and then handing me a sannyāsa-daṇḍa, while sending me out alone in the world just to preach your message and thus be with you always.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for surprising us by going on further to a completely new field-modern, secular India-with your young but now successful movement to show what they were trying so hard not to remember.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for although under the stresses and hardships we all folded up like wooden chairs, you stayed there, year after year, until you had established three wonderful transcendental berths in the harbor of India upon the sea of godlessness, where we could all take shelter and develop more and more, forever.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for journeying in old age to every corner of the earth, bearing the same transcendental message, never changing or compromising; of all your Godbrothers you were the only one to do what Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta wanted, ever injecting the medicine of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and saving the fallen, both big and small.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for wiping away all doubts and defeating all arguments to convince us scientifically of a truth so simple and obvious that we never knew, despite our American education: life comes from life, not from matter, with the conclusion that life can go back to life-such a reassuring realization that removes all the bleak existential bewilderments, giving us the clear, bright light of spiritual truth.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for repeatedly, in every lecture and in every purport, carefully countering and deftly defeating the sly arguments of the impersonalists, who always say the Absolute Truth is one, therefore everything is God (but if everything is God, then God is nothing), who say that they are God but cannot answer how it is that God has come under the influence of māyḍ and forgotten that He is God, who silently meditate, “I am God, you are God, and Kṛṣṇa is not God”-all of them exposed by you as atheists.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for gathering us around you in Vṛndāvana to witness your most intimate pastime, the leaving behind of your body and this world to go back again to Godhead, while we could only cry out like orphans for fear of being left behind in this material world, a wasteland where not one other person on the planet knows Lord Kṛṣṇa as you did.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for leaving behind all your hopes, plans, programs, projects, teachings, zones, deśas, books and even our own disciples, all to develop for lifetimes together; and for somehow ever remaining amongst us to guide and inspire while we try to fulfill your wondersful vision.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for giving us something that one could never dream of even if he could dream for ten thousand years-Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the Absolute Truth.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for making it all possible, because without you there was no hope for this misguided world, nor even for myself, even while some amongst us fell back into the private hells from which we had come.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for teaching me how to thank you and to want to follow your pure example with greater determination this year than ever before, and to try to make the continent of Africa Kṛṣṇa conscious, which you personally told me you wanted to see.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for making me a happy person.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for allowing me to continue to serve you.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you.  
Your spiritual son and servant eternally,

Brahmānanda Swami

## Girirāja Swami

*jaya jaya jahy ajām ajita doṣa-gṛbhīta-guṇāṁ*  
*tvam asi yad ātmanā samavaruddha-samasta-bhagaḥ*  
*aga-jagad-okasām akhila-śakty-avabodhaka te*  
*kvacid ajayātmanā ca carato 'nucaren nigamaḥ*

**“O my Lord, O unconquerable one, O master of all potencies, please exhibit Your internal potency to conquer the nescience of all moving and inert living entities. Due to nescience, they accept all kinds of faulty things, thus provoking a fearful situation. Lord, please show Your glories! You can do this very easily, for Your internal potency is beyond the external potency, and You are the reservoir of all opulence.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****10.87.14)**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

While reading Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta recently, I came across the following passage: “Whosever welfare you desire immediately becomes a Vaiṣṇava, and Kṛṣṇa delivers all Vaiṣṇavas from the reactions of their past sinful activities. You desire the liberation of all kinds of living entities without discrimination. You are very anxious for their good fortune, and I say that simply by your prayer all living entities in the universe can be liberated.” (*Cc. Madhya* 15.169)

When I read these words I remembered the letter you wrote me on my initiation: “I am very glad to understand that you have at least realized some of the transcendental bliss of Krishna consciousness. It is a great fortune for you. Living entities are roaming within the universe in different species of life and in different planetary systems enjoying or suffering the reactions of pious or impious activities. But they have no information how to get out of this entanglement of repetition of birth and death. Due to the darkness of ignorance most living entities have no idea about the value of life. Therefore one who understands the importance of Krishna consciousness must be a very fortunate soul. So I marked it in your person when I was in Boston and I prayed to Krishna that this good soul may be aware of the importance of Krishna consciousness.”

I realized that simply by your prayer I had become “a Vaiṣṇava,” and that by your mercy alone I had been delivered.

Although I have no qualification, I simply pray that you will continue to move me. You are so kind and compassionate! I want you to act through me to deliver more souls. Only then will you and your Lord be satisfied: “All immersed in bliss, sing the glories of Hari. The hearts of the Vaiṣṇavas dance in ecstasy to see this.” (Vaiśiṣṭyāṣṭakam 8)

Your servant,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

*Dear Prabhupāda,*

I offer my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Once again we are celebrating your divine appearance on this planet, and once again I find myself totally inadequate in expressing my debt of gratitude to you. As Uddhava expressed his inability to properly glorify the Supreme Lord, and as Ananta-śeṣa with his many mouths could not describe the transcendental qualities of Kṛṣṇa, similarly, with a million mouths your transcendental qualities and achievements cannot be described. You were always a Vaikuṇṭha man.

*yāṅra citte kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*

**“Even the most learned man cannot understand the words, activities, and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead.” (*****Cc Madhya*** **23.39)**

Sixteen years ago you picked me up from the ocean of nescience and gave me the gift of transcendental vision. By your mercy the divine knowledge of devotional service has been revealed within my heart, bestowing *prema-bhakti* and destroying ignorance. Were it not for you, I would be rotting in the endless cycle of birth and death in different species.

On this auspicious day, I would like to reaffirm my loyalties to your spiritual instructions. You taught us that there are two ways of associating with the spiritual master: *vapuḥ* and *vāṇī*. *Vāṇī* is more important. In dedicating the First Canto to your spiritual master, you stated, “He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him. “I am praying for your mercy so that my love and devotion for your instructions will develop and increase unlimitedly. I pray that I may never deviate from your instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a Vaiṣṇava of the highest order. You are a part of Lord Caitanya’s own spiritual family. You are the personification of determination. You struggled against all odds to establish this Kṛṣṇa consciousness to guide humanity in this dark age of Kali. You gave us the spiritual framework to run the Society. It is by your potency that this movement is growing and increasing daily. You taught us that the Supreme is a sentient being and not impersonal, thereby giving us Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa workship. You taught us how to worship the Lord’s *arcāvigraha* form. You gave us the Bhaktivedanta purports to Śrīla Vyāsadeva and Kṛṣṇa dāsa Kavirāja’s works. Prior to your appearance on this planet their works were not available in the English language. Your Bhaktivedanta purports will be the sole guide to humanity for the next ten thousand years. You taught us the importance of *saṅkīrtana*. You emphasized the importance of book distribution as a means to purify the fallen souls of this age. Just like the leper brāhmaṇa Vāsudeva was cured by Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you have cured millions from the abominable condition of material suffering. Your Bhaktivedanta purports have been converting millions of crowlike men into swans. You have not only cured us of the disease called materialism but also given us a very happy life to live.

You once stated that the disciple can never repay the debt to the spiritual master but can attempt to do so by preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You taught us that to be recognized as a devotee by Kṛṣṇa, one must take to preaching seriously. Because of your tireless efforts, the entire world is now given a chance to be saved from the horrible onslaught of repeated birth and death. You created so many wonderful Vaiṣṇavas, who are sincerely following your instructions and in turn are producing so many first-class grandsons. You gave us the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra as the most important business. The sound of *karatālas* and *mṛdaṅgas* is familiar in cities all over the world. Your gifts cannot be listed. You were perfect and complete in everything you did.

Thank you millions of times for showing me the path of devotional service. Please forgive me for my offenses. Please bestow your mercy so that I can go on working with my Godbrothers and disciples to spread this great movement that you have started. Your causeless mercy is all that I am made of.

Your eternal servant,  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Harikeśa Swami

*My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, my eternal lord and master,*Please accept my most humble obeisances, eternally prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The other night as I was driving from Heidelberg to Frankfurt on my way to a program. I heard a very interesting lecture by Your Divine Grace. While making a point about the performance of one’s duty, you recited a story of a young man who had been very liberally brought up by his aunt. The aunt was very affectionate toward the boy and had given him all possible facilities for his enjoyment; he had whatever he desired. As the boy grew and entered manhood, he fell into very bad company and committed many atrocious crimes, for which he was sentenced to death by the government. As he stood on the gallows at the time of execution, he was asked if he had a last request. He replied that he wished to speak something into the ear of his aunt, who was standing nearby and crying in a piteous voice. It was allowed and the aunt came forward, bending her ear towards his mouth. He then viciously bit her ear off and told her, “If you had not spoiled me, I would not be in this condition I am today!”

The story had left me shaken, as if it was electricity surging through rusty memories. As I sat in the back seat of my car, racing down the German autobahn, I found my thoughts racing even faster, back in time to those days when you so mercifully took me under the shelter of your lotus feet and taught me to serve you. We were just passing by the Frankfurt airport, and my consciousness was soaring beyond the low-hung clouds in the dark sky like a rocket breaking free from the earth’s gravitational pull.

Finally I understood why you had dealt with me as you did. And yet it was so obvious, how could I not have realized it before? Only the most caring, loving father would take so much time and struggle to form and mold a worthless lump of clay as myself into his eternal servant. It is certainly easy to simply rubber-stamp one a servant without actually conquering his heart and soul. But you, my dear master conquered my very heart and soul so completely that I surrendered everything without even knowing what you had done. It is not possible for me to realize the full import of your dealings, as they are still inspiring me to greater levels of love and devotion to your lotus feet.

Now that I look back on it, it could have been that you were taking a risk, treating me so harshly. After all, foolish that I was, I could have completely misunderstood your purpose and rebelled, throwing myself back into the whirlpool of *māyā’s* whims. Of course, that did not happen; it was simply a manifestation of your mercy that you dealt with me hard, just to make me fixed in your loving service without any personal consideration.

I was always very much attached to your personal association . I never wanted to leave it. Those private moments with you in your quarters gave me all the encouragement I needed to remain fixed in your service. But, like all babes in the womb, I had to go. You chastised, “Learning, learning, learning. When are you ever going to do something? Shocking me. When I was your secretary you took every opportunity to make me go back to my preaching mission. At one point you were riding me so fiercely that I crumbled under the pressure and gave up. “I quit,” I boldly pronounced. “You can’t quit,” you immediately retorted, “I fire you!”

Fully crushed, I left your room with no place to go but back to my preaching service. I wanted to hold back the clock so I could pack faster and leave quicker, but the endeavor was becoming impossible, since my mind had already fled to the train station. In this condition, thought was reduced to the phraselike rumblings heard from the clouds before the full-scale thunderstorm.

An ordinary man who simply did not like me would have let me leave without delay. But since you were acting as the perfect father, the real teacher, educating his foolish boy in the realities of life, you called me into your room. “Here is nine hundred dollars. You are now taking a loan from me. You must pay it back with ten percent interest.” I couldn’t believe my ears. You had just finished firing me, throwing me out the door, and now you were handing me nine hundred dollars and demanding that I pay it back with ten percent interest. It was simply inconceivable! I was so bewildered I could only mumble, “I don’t want your money,” as if it had something to do with pride. Again you displayed your total transcendence by ordering me to take it, and there was no other choice. Furthermore, it was due with interest, and there was nothing more to be said.

In an ordinary relationship one could have just run away with it. In this case, of course, that could never have happened. There was no question of simply taking the money and leaving. But on this most intense platform, where master was meeting servant head on it what seemed to be the beginning of a tragedy, the money you held out to me began to represent my very life. Did I want it or not? From your point of view there was no question. From mine, many.

Now someone else was holding back the clock, and I couldn’t even breathe . If I had been capable of fainting I would have, but alas, I was as wide awake as was humanly possible. How to describe the moment? What else could I do? After all, I am your man eternally, I reached out and took the money from your hands. Although I couldn’t look up to see your face, I couldn’t bear it, I knew you were pleased.

This was enough drama for the lifetime, yet it was only one scene in a multi-act play. Who would have tolerated all this? But I did, somehow. Now I am so glad. I needed it; actually I wanted it. I prayed that you would beat my material ideas out of my head. There was no other way, and you did it. Why did you take the time? Why did you care so? No one else in the world would have done so. Why was I so fortunate that I had the great mercy to meet you, to serve you, to give my heart and soul to you? Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you again and again, I am yours eternally. Push me as you will.

I have been appointed by you to act as your representative in North and East Europe. You told me to make it my life, and I have. You told me to print all your books in all the languages of the world. These instructions are dominating my intelligence day and night. I cannot even sit still for a moment without your words reverberating in my head: “Print books! Print books!” All languages, everywhere. There is simply no question of doing anything else. The mission is so concise, so clear, that there is no question of concocting any deviation.

Now I fully understand why you have made me your servant. I will perform your mission no matter what. I am your slave. But is my life so long, the world so accommodating, to allow the completion of this mission? If so, then let me place on your desk in Māyāpur your last unprinted book the day I die.

Your eternal servant,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

The greatest of all  
Devotees of God,  
Please hear our call  
Śrīla Prabhupāda -

Fallen and weak, we  
Have no love for God,  
Please hear our plea  
Śrīla Prabhupāda-

Like thunder your mighty voice  
“we’re not this external form!  
Human life gives us the choice  
to love God, for that we’re born!”

And Kṛṣṇa is God! Kṛṣṇa!  
Supreme Personality  
Of Godhead: Kṛṣṇa….Kṛṣṇa!  
Mere parts-sparks of Him are we!

Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda! Your lotus feet  
Give the shelter of almighty Kṛṣṇa,  
With whom we have vainly tried to compete,  
Entangling ourselves in Māyā’s fishnet.

Save us, Prabhupāda Prabhupāda save me!  
Free us from lust, greed, the madness of pride.  
In loving service to you we are free  
And come at once to the Vaikuṇṭha side.

All glories, all glories to the greatest Prabhu!  
Right-hand man of God on high, almighty Kṛṣṇa-  
Absorb us in your great mission, make us like you,  
Prabhupāda! Bring us to rest at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadiśa Goswami

**Prabhupāda’s Here**

Night for the sleeping,  
Day for the wise,  
Secrets are keeping,  
Not for all eyes.

Energy motion.  
Who’s in control?  
What magic potion?  
Where does it roll?

Mystical being.  
What’s the design?  
What are we seeing?  
What is behind?

Bluffers deceiving,  
Claiming to know.  
Others believing.  
Where will they go?

Why follow blindness?  
Knowledge is near.  
Out of his kindness  
Prabhupāda’s here.

Offering vision,  
Conquering night.  
Hanumān mission,  
Laṅkā alight.

Cheating religion  
Soon to be done.  
Master Confusion  
Better be gone.

“Books are the basis.  
Everything’s there.”  
Knowledge erases  
Darkness and fear.

Purer means faster,  
Aim for the best.  
Kṛṣṇa is master,  
Servants the rest.

Armed with this vision,  
Conquering fear,  
Push on his mission.  
Prabhupāda’s here

Jagadiśa Goswami

## Jagajīvana dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

HE REASONS ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around.  
Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thākura

By estimates of mundane calculation, the termination of one living entity’s sojourn in this temporary body denotes a certain cessation of that particular individual’s pastimes or activities. In his pure state the living entity engaged in transcendental activity knows no such cessation. Being governed by the divine will-*daiviṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ*-his activities may be visible or not on the mundane platform. To demand visibility as a prerequisite for surrender or service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the realm of the neophyte.

“He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.” And as you said, “Everything is in my books.”

So it may be that some sophisticated and scholarly devotees think I am but a fanatical sentimentalist for identifying myself as one of “Prabhupāda’s men,” that I cannot understand the “oneness” of the *guru*; but you had your vision of how to spread and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, a special vision, a successful vision. I am a follower and a worshiper of that vision, that method. I am also a worshiper and follower of those who are worshipers and followers of the special vision. While others, being too much absorbed in philosophical meditations on the Absolute, were playing the Indian *choṭāmṛdaṅga* and “ringing the bells,” you were spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world-playing the *bṛhad* (big) *mṛdanga*, which has not ceased its sound till now.

Of course, I am a coarse fool, unsophisticated and not very intelligent, so it may be that it takes me this whole life to absorb a drop of the ocean of nectar in your books. Others, who are more advanced, who are “beyond” your books-may be they can go on to “higher” topics, to “higher” authorities, but for me, the fallen, simple soul, I can just barely absorb your teachings in your books. Someone once said, “Special, divine power worked within him. So much success is otherwise not possible.” That “special, divine power” is still working through your instructions, through your books, through your representatives and followers. I beg to remain at their lotus feet, at your lotus feet, living with the followers who live by your divine instructions.

I remain,  
Your most fallen servant,  
Jagajīvana dāsa Goswami

## Kirtanānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances. On this auspicious occasion of your appearance we have another occasion to remember you and all the wonderful things you taught us. Before meeting Your Divine Grace we knew nothing; but on the basis of higher authority you have revealed everything, first about the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then about His representative, and finally about ourselves.

It was way back in 1966 that you first told us, “In all the *śāstras* the *guru* is described to be as good as God.” Some freaked out and thought you were another cheap *bhogi*-god; but some stayed on.

Then you gave us a mantra for offering obeisances to the spiritual master and told us, “The disciple’s duty is to offer respect to the *guru* just as he offers respect to God.”

How is this?” someone challenged.

“The *guru* is Kṛṣṇa’s most confidential representative,” you replied, “therefore, he should be offered the same respect one would offer to God.” More freaked. Some left, but some of us began to taste the nectar of living in the very presence of God via His transparent medium.

Your words were not your own, but the reiteration of eternal truth, coming down in an unbroken disciplic succession from Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself:

“One should understand the spiritual master to be as good as I am. Nobody should be jealous of the spiritual master or think of him as an ordinary man, because the spiritual master is the sum total of all demigods.” (*Bhāg.* 11.17.27)

You not only taught us about Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, but also how devotees should relate to each other-in love and trust. You formed an organization, ISKON, and called it your body, and ordered us to work cooperatively for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world: “You will show your love for me by how you cooperate together.”

ISKON is not meant for any other purpose than facilitating our love and trust of each other as you yourself did. If there is no love and trust, everything is finished. Legislation will not affect it, resolutions are useless; meetings are but occasions for political manoeuvring. We can kick men out (you said any idiot can do that), but can we create Vaiṣṇavas? That is the test. That is dependent on *bhakti*. This is the *bhakti* cult, and the *bhakti* must begin in our own house, otherwise we are also in for another “festival of flowers.”

Your insignificant servant.  
Kirtanānanda Swami

## Pañcadraviḍa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

O undaunted savoir, messenger of mercy, emissary of Caitanyadeva,  
Were it not for your presence, the world forlorn would be  
The winds of māyā howl at the window, but the devotees, safe,  
Take refuge in your presence; your instructions for eternity  
Give protection. I surrender myself, and pray to become your eternal servant;  
Were it not for your shelter I would again become but fortune’s knave.  
A slave to my senses, in this material world both alone and depraved,  
But you have picked me up and given me hope, so my most fervent  
Prayer is that you never let me lose sight of your saving lotus feet,  
For your lotus feet have touched the Western lands and given shelter  
To all wayward souls, like myself, who in this world both goodness and evil did meet  
In our wanderings. But now one so magnanimous as you has seen to our welfare.  
And saved us from this world of suffering and illusion, of repeated births,  
Where what we perceived as pleasure is both fleeting and shallow,  
Where the conditioned soul, so foolish and alone, puts faith in his fellow  
Man and gets taken for the proverbial ride, for there is no dearth  
Of cheaters for the cheated. But Kṛṣṇa, my Lord, is so kind  
That where there was once only an empty world of taunting forms,  
There is now the tangible spiritual realm, where the mind  
Is the best friend. You have taken me to that world of the Lord  
And engaged me in His service. O Vaiṣṇava *Ācārya*, eternally perfect,  
Thou hast come to this world but to fan our almost extinguished spark of hope.  
Kindling fire where all was blanketed by the suffocating dense smoke  
Of ignorance. This blazing fire is all-consuming devotional service,  
And as in the forest when there is a great fire the sinful snakes  
Of sinful desires do burn to ashes, unable as they are to flee and escape,  
I, too, am unable to escape you, but this fire my life does not take,  
Only my sins are burned away, and in this blazing effulgence I gaze  
Unto you. More brilliant than the midday sun in Vṛndāvana, you stand  
Before me. No one can conquer me when I am always in the presence  
Of you by following your teachings; I fear no challenge of nescience,  
For I know that this material world doth move but by Kṛṣṇa’s command.  
And you are His most trusted servant; no one can be more dear  
To Him than are you, my eternal father, as your all-encompassing compassion  
Is even greater than the Lord’s, and to all who can see, it is clear  
That you are the Messiah come, the emblem of Bhaktivinoda’s vision.  
My life, most insignificant as it may be, is consecrated to thy service.  
Although I may be surrounded by conditions too rich for Indra,  
Or impoverished, as you were upon your arrival in America.  
In this material world or Vaikuṇṭha, it is my earnest  
Desire that I may someday learn to please you by my actions,  
And in my heart your teachings will be all that I know,  
That everything that you have taught me will be put into practice,  
And that unto this most lonely servitor in separation, a vision of you bestow.  
For it is my supplication that you always accept me, not kick me away,  
Although I have no qualification but to serve your devotees.  
I surrender unto you and fall prostrate with straw in teeth,  
And thus find myself bathed in the sunlight of your most munificent gaze.  
Thou who hast no peer throughout the length and breath of this universe.  
Art the only Lord of my heart, now and beyond when death do us part.  
Only you may have some idea of what is my worth.  
For it is you who in this play of Lord Caitanya’s have given me a start.  
Now that you have picked me up, don’t let me fall again,  
For it is your mercy that has brought me back from dead;  
And give me strength, in the wondrous days that lie ahead,  
That by my guidance others may learn to serve you and hope begin.  
I fall in the dust of your feet, begging for service.

Your fallen servant.  
Pañcadraviḍa Swami

## Prabhupāda-kṛpa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Please allow this wretch to take shelter of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, and please never stop instructing me how to maintain and develop my Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Actually, the process is easy. It is simple and sublime and immediately beneficial. Yet due to so many lusty desires within our hearts we are creating so many difficulties and problems for ourselves. You, however, despite almost insurmountable difficulties externally presented, never had any difficulty internally in remembering your beloved Lord Kṛṣṇa. Thus you remained consistent and steady in the execution of an almost impossible task. By this steadiness and fortitude, this absolute resolution of purpose, you appear as a solid rock upon which the entire ISKON society is built. We could take full shelter in you because we knew you would never fail us. You were so reliable, so predictable in the execution of the principles of devotional service that our faith, which is so weak due to lifetimes of let-downs and disappointments, simply increased more and more in your association.

You were always there when we needed you and always pushing on ahead, taking us (dragging us! ) out into the open, free atmosphere of Vaikuṇṭha Vṛndāvana, out from the murky coverings of false egotism and doubt. In times of need, which were often, we always knew you would be there to help and to console, direct and to guide, and with full vigour and conviction push us, persuade us, chide us, chastise us and somehow or other uplift our flagging spirits, so that we again felt the resolve and enthusiasm necessary for the continual execution of devotional service.

This is still true even now, which makes you appear even all the more wonderful-that in times of trouble and need we only have to invoke your name and loudly cry out for your assistance and immediately you are there to help us weather the stormy seas of illusion and doubt. You are actually already ahead of us in anticipating our needs and arranging for our smooth passage to the spiritual realm, and it is only due to our unfortunate nature that we think of and appreciate you only occasionally.

Lord Kṛṣṇa gives knowledge, remembrance and forgetfulness, and He does it through you. Therefore, I humbly pray that you kindly help me forget my foolish ideas for enjoying in this material world, enlighten me more and more as to my real nature as the eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa, and let me constantly remember your lotus feet and those of Their supreme Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, and thus remain safe from the terrible influence of ignorance. Please let me be always indebted to you and thankful for all the things you have done for me and for all others, and let me somehow or other use every last drop of energy, every last breath in this body, and in all the many bodies I have to come in the future, simply for your glorification. If I can do this, I will then experience some satisfaction and bliss in life; otherwise it will simply be a waste, and not at all worth maintaining. My only anxiety will be that you have done so much, given so much, that whatever I can do or say on your account will be embarrassingly inadequate and not at all worthy of your true glories and qualities. Nevertheless, I know that it will be accepted by you, and the knowledge of this fact is maintaining my life.

We wish to thank you for your gifts  
So freely handed down.  
Your generosity and wish  
We could pay you back somehow,  
With all our hearts we wonder  
How fortunate we are  
That so fallen and so wretched  
You have brought us all so far.

We want to say our thank you  
For prasādam and kīrtana.  
Their Lordships Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa  
And Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.  
It is surely a most wonderful miracle.  
These mlecchas all transformed.  
Who shine like precious ornaments  
When on your form adorned.

We would like to offer our proper thanks  
And thus give our recognition  
That we have appreciated many benefits  
By the execution of your mission.  
It is really so amazing. a miracle.  
How you chose.  
A golden dove from Vaikuṇṭha,  
To associate with crows.

Thank you for your mercy books.  
For your temples. for a place to live;  
Thank you for revealing that .  
True pleasure comes only to those who give.  
And just as you came to give us pleasure,  
For others we shall do the same,  
For the lost and fallen may be rescued.  
By the chanting of your name.

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya, Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda !

Your fallen and forgetful servant,  
Prabhupāda-kṛpa Goswami

## Rāmeśvara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Lord Kṛṣṇa,*

Please guide me in glorifying and serving my eternal *guru.* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

PLEASE accept me as your servant, birth after birth. After tasting the nectar of your transcendental service, I only want to be your servant. Liberation from the material world is not a specific desire for your real servant. I beg to become your real servant. A real servant will never cheat. A real servant will never forget his spiritual vows. A real servant will never fall down. By your mercy, he will, with great conviction, give up sex desire (once and for all). A real servant will always be fully engaged in your mission. A real servant will become a pure preacher and see all souls as his family. A real servant will change the material world and elevate the fallen souls by giving them your mercy. He will give the world the real hope which you have established in this world. In spite of your perfect humility, a real servant cannot check himself from making the entire world accept you as its savior. You have established the chanting of the holy names. You have established the transcendental knowledge of *Bhagavad-gitā Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam. The Nectar of Devotion.* and Śrī *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* for every soul to read and be taught, for ten thousand years. You have established real worship of the Deity. You have established the perfect human diet through massive *prasādam* distribution. You have established the spiritual city of Śrīdhām Māyāpur and the *varṇāśrama* system for the entire materialistic society to adopt. You have established the transformation of the entire earth planet into a Vaikuṇṭha planet. You have established the universal path back to home, back to Godhead, and thus you have delivered all conditioned souls who take birth on this planet: All these things are factually established, and their worldwide external manifestation is simply separated by a little time.

*You* give your *darśana* to your devotees at your *samādhi.* You accept their pleading and begging to remain your eternal servants. You force them to correct their deviations as part of the criterion for accepting them into your eternal entourage. You are with them when they go forward into the battle against materialistic voidism and impersonalism as your preachers, your book distributors. You sustain them as full-time preachers for the entirety of this life, and also future lives. You have given your real servants the full understanding and experience of selfless love. No one else could give the taste of real love in such an intense way.

*Your* real servant has received the greatest benediction of Lord Kṛṣṇa. He never forgets your instructions. He continually goes on tasting love you. He does not talk of cheap sentiment. All his actions personify his love. He is your loving devotee, working day and night with only one goal-to attain the position of your eternal servant. Others may think of their eventual *rasa* with Kṛṣṇa; your real servant doesn't even have time such thoughts. He is fully satisfied to just be at your lotus feet, wherever you may take him. For your real servant, that is the ultimate.

I am a disappointment; unworthy, fallen. My prayer is for causeless mercy, because despite all my disqualifications, I truly want to become your real servant one day. Please don't ever separate yourself from this lowly, aspiring servant.

Your unworthy servant,  
Rāmeśvara Swami

## Rūpānuga dāsa

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I am honored to be your disciple. You are a founder- *ācārya,* the most rare Vaiṣṇava, who appears once in a great while to begin a spiritual dynasty that lives forever. It lives forever because what you have begun here continues on into the spiritual world.

There are many amazing things in this world. The spirit soul is amazing; the fact that people act as if they are going to live forever is amazing; the activities of my sincere Godbrothers are amazing; the teachings of the six Gosvāmīs are amazing; the timely appearances and pastimes of Nārada Muni are amazing; the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is amazing; but the most amazing thing is that you continue allowing me to serve you.

It is easier to understand the intricacies of śāstr than to understand your mercy upon *me-guru-kṛpā.* I can conceive of practically anything but this. Over the years I carefully maintain my fallen position and still you give me your blessings daily. I could live the rest of my life on one drop, but still you pour torrents of your blessings upon me.

And I am always amazed at how you are giving me some new duty, and I pray that 1 appreciate each such opportunity as if it were my last.

I am simply a dying man, my life two-thirds over, praying that you keep me a little longer. You have honored me by accepting me as your disciple. Now my life is to honor you.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Lately, devotional service appears to me to be getting more difficult. But I know this is a test. As you write in your *Bhagavad-gītā* purport, "One must be able to pass the test of the spiritual master, and when he sees the genuine desire of the disciple, he automatically blesses the disciple with genuine spiritual understanding." (Bg. 4.34)

The demons *(āsurīṁ bhāvam āśritam)* are becoming more active against us. It is a real war of devotees versus demons, and although we can always expect victory, as promised by Lord Kṛṣṇa, sometimes we ha*ve* to sustain losses in money and even men. It may be hard, but it is clear what you want; we have to always fight, resisting our enemies and positively, peacefully spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

More painful than battling the *asuras* is when fighting occurs among your disciples. This happens when someone wrongly refuses to accept the ultimate of ISKCON and the GBC. As Kṛṣṇa’s final instruction in the *Bhagavad-gītā* is "Give up all religious duties and surrender to Me," I take it as a final instruction when you said, "Your love for me will be shown when you cooperate together." Or as our Godbrother Śrīla Kīrtanānanda Swami recently said, "The unity of ISKCON is the highest worship of Prabhupāda." To cause lack of faith in ISKCON or to attempt to split or harm ISKCON means going directly against your heart's desire. Your sincere followers will always work to keep your movement intact. Serious deviation from this order causes intense pain to the body of ISKCON. But again it is clear what we must do-preserve the unity and purity at all costs. Sometimes it seems my own path also is becoming more difficult. My duties sometimes seem to be a heavy burden. But the remedy is clear-I must push on in service and seek relief at your lotus feet, at the feet of the Vaiṣṇavas, and in the cool nectar rays of chanting and hearing.

Regarding apparent difficulties that occur in devotional service, you have written, "Sometimes we may superficially see that a devotee is in difficulty because of being engaged in devotional service, but the fact is different. When a devotee suffers for Kṛṣṇa, that suffering is transcendental enjoyment. Unless someone becomes a devotee, this cannot be understood." (Purport, *Bhag.* 10.8.51)

And regarding disturbances in ISKCON, you have written, ". ..We should not become disturbed by so-called disturbances. There have been so many disturbances to our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, but we cannot give up our forward march. On the contrary, people are receiving his movement very enthusiastically all over the world, and they are purchasing literature about Kṛṣṇa consciousness with redoubled energy . "Thus there are both encouragements and disturbances. This was so even in Kṛṣṇa's time." (Purport, *Bhāg.* 10.11.27)

Therefore, on your Vyāsa-pūjā day I pray to be always engaged in your holy service in ISKCON. Admitting my weak-heartedness, I pray to you to make me strong enough to never abandon my duties, because whoever will stay with you and serve you sincerely will be victorious over all obstacles. He will see the glorious victory of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in this world and will gain blissful realization as the eternal servant of you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

**He Opens Our Darkened Eyes**

I jivātmā, submit myself.  
Blind and destitute,  
Ignorant of my origin,  
I stumbled from birth to birth  
Seeking an identity.  
I remain unknown,  
A stranger to the world,  
Willo '-the-wisp actor  
In the shadow play of life.  
fathers, mothers, brothers, friends  
All embrace me  
On the withering bosom of time,  
A family of the blind.

I jivātmā, wandered end lessly  
The path of despair,  
When again  
You found me  
After crores of births.  
With select words  
You restored my sight,  
Revealed my identily  
As Gods eternal servant,  
By your touch  
Removed all weariness  
And dressed me anew,  
Led me to a secluded place and said-

"Listen, *jīva*. I am your dearmost friend, come to take you back home. Don't you remember? That place from which you strayed immemorial births ago? Don't you recall that all-attractive person, playing upon His flute, with eyes like blooming lotuses, with a peacock feather adorning His head and His threefold-bending form captivating all who see Him? That Supreme Personality, surrounded by cows and calves, who is being served with unlimited affection by the residents in Vṛndāvana is now waiting for you. That youthful boy, Kṛṣṇa, who is beyond compare, is calling you to return. Come with me. Let us not delay even for a moment."

How much I wanted to go with you at that very moment. But from a distance I heard the faint voice- of those dear to me: father and mother, brothers, sisters, and friends, imploring me not to leave them. How could I neglect their fervent appeal? So I asked your indulgence while I went to try to console them.

First my father, *buddhi* (intelligence), spoke up: "O son, are you leaving us today? We who have been joined together for so long, through happiness and distress, are we now to be separated? I have always maintained you, as well as your brothers and sisters, while teaching you to be discerning. By proper remembrance you have always fared well, while sometimes due to misapprehension you have caused us all to suffer. Are you now making the proper choice?”

Then my brothers *kriyā śaktiḥ* (the senses of action) spoke: "O our brother, hear our words. Together we have diligently worked to improve our family's condition, and gradually we have increased our position both qualitatively and numerically. While moving here and there, we have always tried to serve you, for you are the foremost amongst us. Without your presence we would have no one to glorify by either our words "or actions."

Next my sisters *vājñāna-śakti* (the senses of acquiring knowledge) offered these appealing words: "O brother, contemplating your imminent departure makes us think that we must have committed some grievous offense to have deserved such a fate. As members of the fair sex, we are naturally prone to enjoyment. While acquiring knowledge of the phenomenal world, we have savored the fruits of all varieties of worldly pleasures, but we have always shared everything with you. Perhaps we have made a mistake by misrepresenting something to you, or is it simply that you can no longer tolerate our impetuous natures? or do you feel satiated due to overindulgence? In any case, we request you to stay amongst us, for without you we would surely dry up and die."

The friends of my brothers and sisters, *mahā-bhūtāni* and *pañcabhiḥ* (the gross and subtle elements), now spoke in turn: "O best of the personalities, please hear our prayer. Though we have never associated personally with you, we are most intimately connected to all of your family members. Indeed, it is we alone who have actually supplied them all the necessities of life. Thus indirectly we have always been your benefactor. In truth, we may state that we live only to fulfill your desires. If all our friends, your family members, perish in the fire of separation, then we also will no longer have cause for existence. Please therefore continue to give life to all those dear persons who so much adore you."

Hearing all these beseeching words, my mother, *manaḥ* (the mind), could not restrain herself any longer, and weeping pitiously spoke as follows: "O my dear son, how can you cause your father, brothers, sisters, and friends such pain? Don't you see how they love you? Even before your birth I desired all auspiciousness for you. Like a tortoise who hatches her eggs simply by meditation, so my constant thoughts have brought you forth into the world. You always have been such a faithful son. Though my condition has not been steady, nevertheless you have attended to each of my thoughts and reflections and made their fulfillment your own desire. Now in my old age, when I cannot think clearly, surely you will not abandon me?” And with these words my mother cried even more pitiously.

When all who had spoken remained silent, one amongst them now approached. It was *ahaṅkāra* (false ego), who like my own double had wandered with me from birth to birth. "Friend," he began, putting his hand around my shoulder. "We have known each other for a long time. But never have I seen you in such a mood, indifferent even to those so close to you. Whenever I have helped to rehearse you for the variety of roles you have had to play through birth and death, lifetime to lifetime, always you have been attentive to every detail. Yet now suddenly you seem so detached. Something very extraordinary must have befallen you. Not since the time of our first acquaintance, when you chose to befriend me, giving up your actual home and family, have I seen you in such a state. At the time of declaration of your self-willed independence you were entrusted to my care. And now even I seem unable to appeal to you. I can only conclude that you must have met someone from your original home who has once again reminded you of your original consciousness."

Then suddenly you reappeared, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and taking me aside said, "My dear *jīva,* my dear son, please do not become bewildered by the words of family and friends. After all, they are blind. What do they know of transcendental life? Now, by dint of your liberated consciousness, you are no longer obliged to perform the ordinary duties of social intercourse. Your time should be spent only for cultivating devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By continuous engagement in the loving service of the Lord, all that is inauspicious will be done away with, and in such a pure condition you will be able to join the Supreme Lord once again and participate in His eternal pastimes." Thus you spoke to me with great love and concern and implored me to come with you. But still I could not entirely forget my attachment to all those near to me. Without a word, you immediately understood my thoughts and again spoke.

"My son, let your mind be at rest. The conflict which you are feeling is apparent only, not real. Just try to understand that you are already liberated. As such, you are fully empowered to open the eyes of others as I have done for you. Simply you must engage them all in acts of devotion to the transcendent Lord, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Then in a purified condition they too will be able to behold the spiritual world unfolding before them.

"Specifically, your father, *buddhi,* should have no doubt in my words. They are the conclusion of all revealed scriptures. Your father must realize that he is not the body. When he has fully understood his constitutional position as pure spirit soul, then only can he be a real father for your brothers and sisters.

"Regarding your sisters, *śrotram* should engage in hearing continuously the glories of the Lord. *Tvak* should engage in serving exalted Vaiṣṇavas. *Dṛk* should always meditate on the form of the Deity within the temple. *Rasanā* should respect the Lord's transcendental remnants and never, ever contemplate food not first offered. *Nāsikā* should smell the incense, sandalwood and *tulasī* offered to the Lord. Thus your sisters, the senses, will be fully satisfied, and their lusty natures will be fully controlled by absorption in the service of the Lord.

"Your brothers should be actively engaged in the Lord's service. As regards to *vāk.* he should always glorify Kṛṣṇa with chosen Vedic prayers. *Karau* should perform the service of the hands, which are so many in variety. *Caraṇau* should travel to the temples of the Lord. *Meḍhram* should engage in sex life according to religious vows. And *pāyu* should observe proper dieting and bathing to always remain clean. In this way your brothers will all remain happy, and your family will prosper along with their friends.

"As regards to your mother, *manaḥ.* she should engage in meditation by always remembering the name, form, pastimes and qualities of the Lord. Then she will become fixed in trance and pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness. "

After delivering these instructions, you smiled very mercifully. You had not rejected me because I could not give up my attachments and surrender all at once. I felt so very thankful that I fell down to the ground and embraced your lotus feet and washed them with the tears falling from my eyes.

Suddenly you were gone, and though I looked everywhere I could not find you. Then in a stunned condition I realized what had happened. You had asked me to come with you, but I had not been ready, and so you have left me behind after restoring my vision. And now realizing that I had lost your personal guidance, tears poured incessantly out of my wide-open eyes. In the distant darkness I saw, standing with their arms outstretched, all of my family members, blind, waiting for your instructions.

Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami
