## Vṛndāvana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We beg to offer our most humble obeisances prostrated at your lotus feet. We would like to thank you eternally, again and again, for giving us the most wonderful opportunity to serve you personally at your own home in Vṛndāvana-dhama. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu came to Vṛndāvana and saw that Vṛndāvana was covered over by jungles due to the influence of time, so He ordered His intimate disciples and associates to come to Vṛndāvana and excavate holy places and build temples in honor and glorification of Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, when you came to Vṛndāvana, after taking *sannyāsa,* you saw how Vṛndāvana was also , becoming more and more filled with worldly men. So it was your desire to have an ISKCON temple. You said, " A simple temple just like .Govindajī’s," but Kṛṣṇa gave you so much more. He gave you a wonderful palace where you could worship your own personal Deities of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. He gave you a wonderful *gurukula,* so your grandchildren could be brought up properly under the *brahmacāri* system. And now, just like a wonderful diamond nestled in the midst of a jeweled locket, your marble *samādhi-mandira* is being established, where you will remain eternally situated in your ecstatic *samādhi* after reentering your *nitya-līla* eternal pastimes with Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. For so many years and years, so many travelers all traversed the traditional road from Delhi to Agra to see the Taj, leaving Vṛndāvana unnoticed. But now, because you have manifested yourself in this wonderful form of ISKCON Vṛndāvana, everyone is taking a detour on Chattikara Road in order to see the wonderful Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira, the " Angleji Mandir" ("English temple"). Call it what they may, it is simply you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In your sacred rooms between the guest house and the temple, where you departed in 1977, thousands of people are coming daily to take your *darśana.* They see your wonderful form sitting behind the desk dictating *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and after a few minutes of amazement and wonder, they ask, "Isn't Swamiji speaking today?" And we smilingly answer, *"mūrti he!" Then* they affirm, *" mūrti he!"as* they continue by, offering their prostrated obeisances, understanding that there is no difference between you and your *mūrti* form. Thousands and thousands of them come to see your room, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Simple pilgrims, tourists, government officials, and even kings and queens have paraded through your rooms and admired your beautiful form dictating on the dictating machine. They have seen how the *Bhagavad-gitā* has been published in so many languages-English, Spanish, French, German, Arabic, Japanese, Hebrew, and even Chinese. Seeing your books they become filled with reverence and admiration at your glories.

Then they proceed to the temple, to the wonderful palace of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, Gaura-Nitāi, and Rādhā-Śyāma. The villagers look at the beautiful chandeliers hanging in the *darśana-maṇḍapa* and ask, "Swamiji, are these made out of real diamonds?" Or if the lights are off because it is during the noon ā*rati,* they ask, "Swamiji, at what time will the chandeliers be giving *darśana* tonight?" Then they look at your beautiful form sitting on the *vyāsāsana,* ready to give the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and they ask, "Is it by your American technology that you have preserved *Guru*jī's body?"

The evening time comes, and the *sandhyā-ārati* begins. They see the faces of all colors and all races joining the *saṅkīrtana* of the holy names of Hari, and they become simply more and more amazed. The devotees, the *kīrtana,* the cleanliness, the chandeliers, the Deities and Their beautiful clothes (you once told Oṁkāra to dress all of the Deities every day, and now, by your divine instructions that is being fulfilled)-at every corner the visitors simply become more and more amazed. Everything becomes a manifestation of your transcendental instructions, and everyone becomes amazed.

Prabhupāda, you are the ambassador of India to the rest of the world, and ISKCON Vṛndāvana is the United Nations of the spiritual world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can understand that by your transcendental *vānī* you are forever existing in Vṛndāvana, and you are even more present than ever. We beg at your lotus feet to allow us to remain at your service eternally in the holy *dhāma.* Many of us are unqualified *mlecchas* from very lowly backgrounds, who have no right to enter into Vṛndāvana *dhāma.* But you are so kind to us, to your children and to your grandchildren, that you have made a special little area of Vṛndāvana in Ramaṇa-retī where we can serve you with all humility and respect, and still get the benefit of living in the holy *dhāma.* Only by great souls like you can these special concessions be made, and we thank you again and again for your divine grace.

You asked us not to leave this complex, but to remain always within the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength to remain humble, prostrated at your lotus feet, simply serving you in this one little section of Vṛndāvana. Without your grace we don't want to know one square inch of Vṛndāvana outside of this complex. We simply want to remain eternally in this transcendental boat that you have built for us, right in the holy *dhāma.* In your books you have stated that you established this Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira to show the rest of the world that the worship of Gaura-Nitāi and the worship of Kṛṣṇa- Balarāma are one and the same. You have also stated that you have built this temple in order to provide proper shelter for the Europeans and Americans and those from all parts of the world who come to Vṛndāvana to receive proper instruction on the science of *bhakti-yoga*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please forgive our offenses due to our neophyteness and lack of transcendental taste, and keep us prostrated in the glory of the dust of your lotus feet for ever and ever in this holy place.

Your servants at Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira

## Vṛndāvana Gurukula

**Śrīla Prabhupāda-caturdaśakam**

Glory to thee, Śrī Gurudeva,  
I prostrate at thy feet;  
This poor soul, lord, I beg thee save-  
Bestow thy mercy sweet!

The name divine you proffered me;  
I took Him by your grace;  
I'd faith His name would chasten me  
And bowed before thy face.

I pray to Kṛṣṇa earnestly  
To let me know thee more;  
This wish doth grow more fervently  
Than it hath done before.

O master, pray, when shall I chance  
To see you as you are?  
Right jubilant I then shall dance  
And love thee more by far.

O Prabhupāda, stay by me close  
And never quit my heart;  
I see death swing his ghastly noose  
This corpse and soul to part.

You're likened, lord, unto a cloud  
That pours forth cooling rain;  
Please quench this lust-I am too proud-  
And end this age-old pain.

You're e'er immersed in mellows sweet  
And chanting Kṛṣṇa s name;  
The glories of thy lotus feet  
Shall bring thee endless fame.

You have us serve the Deity  
And cleanse His house as well-  
Such joy to serve the moiety  
And scape the snare of hell!

Prasādam sweet each day we taste  
That we'd not done before;  
By thee, dear lord, we thus are graced  
Whom prouder saints abhor.

Śrī Rādhikā-Mādhava's names  
You ever glorify.  
You praise Their forms; Their loving games  
Thy soul doeth magnify.

You daily serve the Divine Pair  
In Vraja’s secret groves:  
Most sacred service, sans compare,  
Assistant to Their loves.

Thou art Lord naris servitor  
And honored equally.  
Who amongst men doth merit more?  
I bow again to thee.

By your good grace alone one gets  
The mercy of the Lord;  
At dawn, at noon and as sun sets,  
Your needs must be adored.

Dear Prabhupāda, I've thus declared  
Thy glories but in part;  
Without thy kindness I'd despaired  
And borne an empty heart !

*Your servants at Vṛndāvana Gurukula,*

Dhanurdhāra Swami / Rūpa-vilāsa dāsā / Ananta-rūpa dāsā / Nayanābhirāma dāsa / Karṇāmṛta dāsa / Pūrṇacandra dāsa / Balarāma dāsā / Muktipada dāsā / Bhūrijana dāsā / Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī / Sita-devī dāsī / Candrikā-devī dāsī / Jagattāriṇī-devī dasi. Jaya *Guru*pāda

**Preaching**

Raghunātha dāsa / Viśvaretāḥ dāsa

## Washington, D.C.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept the following humble offering on your all-auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day.

**Guru-pūja Meditations**

*Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma:*

Your lotus feet were never touched  
Although the earth you seemed to tread;  
I pray I'II always have the chance  
To place those feet upon my head.

*kevala- bhakati-sadma:*

Pure love for Kṛṣṇa is sheltered there,  
Protected by the house they've made;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To soothe my soul within their shade.

*bando mui sāvadhāna mate:*

Repeatedly forced to bow to death,  
And birth, old age, disease,  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To bow before your feet with ease.

*Jāhāra prasāde bhāi:*

Everything has come from this-  
Your causeless grace to one so low;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To spread that gracefor all to know.

*e bhava toriyā jāi:*

Deep in the ocean with other fools,  
You saved me-that I can't forget;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To try to pay my eternal debt.

*Kṛṣṇa-prāpti hoy jāhā ha 'te:*

I didn't know He was the goal,  
But now you've made it crystal clear;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To serve the one you hold so dear.

*guru-mukha-padma vākya:*

Words of guidance flow from your mouth  
Like nectar from a lotus flower;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To strain my ears to catch that shower.

*cittete koriyā aikya:*

vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ ...  
This verse became your life and soul;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To make your mission my only goal.

*ār nā koriho mane āśā:*

There is no room for others now;  
My mind is filled with thoughts of you;  
I pray I’ll never have the chance  
To try to look for someone new.

*Śrī-guru-caraṇe rati:*

I spent my life attached to things  
That left me empty, full of grief  
I pray I’ll always have the chance  
To clutch your feet for real relief

*ei se uttama-gati:*

The highest goal is nothing more,  
Than to serve your feet with every breath;  
I pray I’ll always have the chance  
To serve them-even after death.

*je prasāde pūre sarva āśā:*

I need not worry about my fate;  
By serving you all goals I’ll reach;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To imbibe this truth the śāstras teach.

*cakhu-dān dilo jei:*

My eyes were blind with hate and lust;  
I saw the world from my frog well;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To shine your light within my cell.

*janme janme prabhu sei:*

For many lives you 've come for me  
To pull me from my wretched plight;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To grasp your hand without a fight.

*divya-jñān hṛde prokāśito:*

Within my heart you lit the flame,  
The truth that burns away the fog;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To know this truth-that I'm your dog.

*prema-bhakti jāhā hoite:*

The sun shines forth with heat and light  
But from you beams a different ray;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To taste that prema you give away.

*Avidyā vināśa jāte*

As darkness flees before the sun  
All ignorance is chased by you;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To keep your form within my view.

*vede gāya jāhāra carito:*

While dogs and hogs sing of polished beasts,  
The Vedas sing in praise of you;  
I pray I'll always have the I chance  
To praise you, Lord, in all I do.

*Śrī-guru karuṇā-sindhu*

O Gurudeva, your mercy flows  
To even those who try to hide;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To drown myself within that tide.

*adhama janāra bandhu:*

The friend of all the fallen souls,  
You've proved it now for all to know;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To let that friendship grow and grow.

*Prabhupāda lokera jīvana:*

You came to give me back my life,  
Yet when you left I felt alone;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To realize that you've never gone.

*Hā hā prabhu koro doyā:*

Without your mercy this soul laments;  
There's nothing in this world but pain;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To stand within your mercy rain.

*deho more pada-chāyā*

In the cooling shade of your lotus feet,  
A universe of souls can fit;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To enter that shade and stay in it.

*ebe jaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana:*

The ISKCON world now tries to spread  
Your wondrous glories to every town;  
I pray I'll always have the chance  
To be an ISKCON man and not a clown.

*Begging to remain,*  
*Your servants at New Hastināpura Dhāma*

## Wellington, N.Z.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Never met you, my lord, not personally; yet you *are* living on the tongues of your disciples who love you so dearly. From them I have heard-

Your life, an offering of love to Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your words, thoughts and deeds, dedicated to Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your heart of compassion for those forgetful of Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your sadness for the sufferings of those inimical to Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your pleasure found in the pleasure of Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your happiness in seeing someone engage in service to Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your unswerving determination to print books about Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your expertise in constructing temples of Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your carefully trained disciples, devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
Your mission, to make the world conscious of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, our savior, guide, friend, teacher, chastiser , father, well-wisher ...You are everything to us!

*Your disciples and granddisciples,*

Subala dāsa / Parivādī dāsa / Trilokeśvara dāsa / Ananta-deva dāsa / Pāda-padma dasa / Śubha-śuci-devī dāsī / Rukmiṇī-devī dāsī. Govinda

## West Germany

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

That which distinguishes the Vaiṣṇava movement from what is commonly known as "religion" is precisely that person to whom we offer praise on this glorious day: the *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee of the Lord, the pure representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, who has descended out of his causeless mercy simply to give us his transcendental association and thus save us from our own nescience.

The religions of the world are nothing more than colossal facades, whose splendid doorways promise entrance to *bhukti* or *mukti* in exchange for the ritual acceptance of dogma at a nominal fee. Once inside, however, the initiate is confronted with the hollow emptiness of a tomb; instead of finding salvation, he is again gripped by hellish material desires, which force him to continue in the animalistic pursuits of meat-eating, illicit sex, gambling and intoxication. His illusion is compounded by the soothing prayers and false promises of his so-called spiritual authorities, who assure him that he is fulfilling his duty to God and need not worry any longer over the meaning of his existence.

The pure devotee of the Lord appears to show such unfortunate souls the unalloyed way back home, back to Godhead. He therefore boldly denounces pretentious religiosity as being a hindrance, not a help, along the path of advancement. He has no concord with dogma, for dogma is practically as untouchable as a dead body, being food only for vulture-like men. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the living message of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, transmitted by His merciful servants and incarnations in accordance with time, place and circumstance. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is vital, dynamic, revolutionary. It never submits to the vain ideals of materialistic people. It is never diverted by the fawning attentions of society, friendship and love which have become the bedfellows of the worldly religions. The pure devotee of the Lord is the embodiment *(vapuḥ)* of the teachings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness (vāṇī); as such, his mission is not to flatter us and thus increase our infatuation with sensual affairs, but to abruptly sever us from our material entanglements, even if it is against our will. Thus he is known as *sādhu* ("he who cuts").

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one bogus *guru* of the neo-Hindu persuasion has said of you, "He is the angel of death." Though he undoubtedly meant to offend you, we think that mother Sarasvatī has spoken your praises through his lips *(sei śabde sarasvatī karena stavana).* You are indeed the angel of death for the foolish *kali-chela* authorities who poke their heads arrogantly out of the mire of the modern degraded culture to offer counsel to others. Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself declares that He is known as *mṛtyu* (death) in this world; it is indeed a compliment for Kṛṣṇa's servant to be similarly designated.

But the pure devotee of the Lord, though he is as uncompromising as a thunderbolt, is nonetheless the enemy of no one and the well-wisher of all. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your only purpose is to introduce us to our dearmost friend, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. To accomplish this, you must brush aside the debris of mundane chatter which passes as religion and philosophy and prepare a nice sitting place for the worshipable Lord Kṛṣṇa to appear. All those who volunteer to lend a hand in your service to Kṛṣṇa are immediately awarded with the nectarean taste of the pure love of God which you are always relishing. But those who oppose the establishment of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this world perceive both the Lord and Your Divine Grace as all-devouring death come to disrupt their sweet dreams of happiness. Such is the foolishness of the stubborn materialist: that which is nectar he takes to be poison, and the real cause of death-attachment to the material body-he embraces as nectarean.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is your continuing merciful presence that is sustaining the devotees and this movement, especially in the face of opposition from demons. The whole aim of our service is to simply enter deeper and deeper into your merciful presence. We hanker for nothing else in this world. We hope never to idly bask in the limelight of mundane success; in fact, we pray that there may always be opposition to this movement on the preaching front, simply so that we can always take shelter of your lotus feet as children surrendering to the father. Please give us strength to carry on. This is all we ask.

*Your servants in the West German temples.*

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back To Godhead

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
We think of you  
In Delhi, on the street,  
In homes and offices,  
Distributing your Back to Godhead.  
In the cold  
You had no coat or cādar  
In the heat one day  
You fainted.  
In the mornings  
You sometimes fasted,  
Walking to the printer  
To proofread your one-page paper:  
Eternal message of the Vedas  
In a 1956 tabloid  
We remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Speaking out,  
Walking with your disciples  
In parks and gardens  
And on beaches,  
Preaching and preaching,  
Exposing atheism in places  
We'd never suspected,  
Encouraging us to preach boldly,  
Leaving your shoe-prints and  
cane-prints in the sand  
We want to serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda  
By making Back to Godhead  
Bigger than Time or Life,  
By helping to bring your same  
Bold, eternal preaching,  
Your humor and camaraderie,  
To the streets of 1983,  
'84, '85, '86…  
Your kind and  
Stern instructions,  
Transcendental vision  
Bhāgavata scholarship,  
Uncompromising purity,  
Cutting (chopping) commentary  
And gentlemanly demeanor in  
All of this  
Embodied in Back to Godhead,  
In your words and  
The words of your followers,  
We want to see  
On the streets,  
In homes and offices,  
Schools and factories,  
On conference tables  
And restaurant counters,  
In everyone’s hand and  
On everyone’s mind-  
That’s what we want to see.  
Please let us take part  
In your ISKCON movement,.  
Serving you and your  
Disciples and grand disciples,  
Bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
Into every sphere of life  
In every town and village.

*Your servants at Back to Godhead magazine,*

Maṇḍaleśvara dāsa / Draviḍa dāsa / Yamarāja dāsa / Ādi-keśava dāsa / Śubhānanda dāsa / Mathureśa dāsa / Darpahā dāsa / Tattva-vit dāsa / Viṣṇu-jvara dāsa / Bali Mahārāja dāsa / Viśākhā-devī dāsī / Kuntī-devī dāsī / Janeśvarī-devī dāsī

## Bhaktivedanta Institute

You gave us inconceivable maps, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just to guide us out of the material world. These maps, the personified scriptures, were not discernable to anyone. You breathed life into them-your life-and then presented them to the world, as gifts at your own expense.

We were the few forlorn travelers, unfit, proud children of the dark Kali-yuga womb, who became attracted to your matchless gifts by the grace of providence. You gathered us together, clothed us, fed us, bathed us, doctored us and set us moving toward the destination.

Then you left us. You left us with your gifts: your books, your memories and the company of your children.

Whoever thinks that these inconceivable gifts are replaceable is certainly making a grave mistake. Neither your books, nor your memories, nor the association of your disciples are replaceable with other books, other memories, other association. If we take any of these gifts, although matchless and free, too cheaply, exploiting them for our personal use, we will soon find ourselves aimlessly wandering again.

But if we treat each of your gifts as precious items, respecting their unique qualities and individuality, then your desire to distribute these gifts throughout the universe will be done.

Let us bow our heads down to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In the mood of submissive service to you, let us offer each one of the matchless gifts you have given us a cushioned seat and a receptive ear within the core of our hardened hearts.

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in San Francisco*

## ISKCON Cinema

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

May our feelings for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, be completely free from material tinge. Then only will they withstand the test of time.

Insidious, unrelenting, omnipresent time-it destroys only material things; it cannot affect the spiritual. Pure love for you will not only remain immune to time's influence, but will also grow ever deeper and fresher.

We know we don't deserve such a boon. Yet it is our humble prayer. Please do not neglect us.

*Your servants,*

Yadubara dāsa, Viśākhā-devī dāsī, Amṛta

## Ministry of Public Affairs

*Our most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

As the prime benediction of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement, your pastimes Included:

Coming to the West, with just your Bhāgavatam editions,  
You showed preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

Starting out in New York City, a place gruesome as perdition,  
You showed preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

You prescribed the holy name, like a spiritual physician;  
You showed preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

Challenged all the modern myths, by exposing false positions,  
You showed preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

Preaching Gītā as it is, not diluting the tradition,  
You showed preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

Founding saṅkīrtana and temples, with infallible precision,  
You showed preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

Now although you've gone from sight, your movement shows no diminution;  
You show preaching rests on bhakti, not material conditions.

*Your servants,*

**International Ministry of Public Affairs**
